ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-14-senat-45-3.u2	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen of the Senators! I on the Law on the System of General Courts, request that it not be placed on the agenda. We discussed this at the Senior Convention meeting, but the Marshal did not take into account the request of representatives of the Civic Platform Club. This is a crazy dangerous bill. This is a law by which a coup d'état is carried out in Poland. This is a law by which independent judiciary in Poland is liquidated. This is a bill that we all know in this Chamber is breaking the constitution, only some people are talking about it out loud, and others won't admit it. This law in at least 3 places violates the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, thus changing the state system in Poland. This law violates the article. 173 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which states that I quote The courts and tribunals shall be separate and independent from the other authorities. This means that the courts and tribunals are to be separated from the legislative authority and the executive authority. Meanwhile, due to the provisions of this law, they will cease to be. This law undermines the division of power in Poland. This law undermines the constitution in its Article. Point (a) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 1, which also speaks of the independence of judges. If the independence of the courts is undermined, it will affect the independence of the judges. There's no way it won't work. Therefore, the judges will cease to be in office without notice. And the constitution says that the judges in their office are unimpeachable. Finally, most importantly, this law undermines one of the fundamental human rights enshrined not only in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, but also in international acts ratified by the Republic of Poland. In the European Convention on Human Rights and in the Convention The UN on human rights. Art. 170... art. 45 of the Constitution says, "I quote." Everyone has the right to an independent, impartial and independent court to consider the case. And when the courts are subject to political power, they will cease to be independent, and so citizens will lose. They belong naturally to them and by virtue of the law The right to an independent judicial judgment. That is why I ask, Mr. Marshal, that this law should not be included on the agenda, because it places the Senate under the responsibility of For violating the Constitution. All those in favor of this bill will do so with the knowledge that they are breaking the constitution. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-14-senat-45-3.u30	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I would like to present a report by the Committee on National Economy and Innovation on the Act on the Promotion of the Polish Economy by the Polish Investment and Trade Agency, adopted by the Sejm on 7 July 2017. The Senate Marshal sent the bill to the committee on 10 July. The Commission considered the proposed draft law at its meeting on 12 July 2017. This law sets out the rules for the performance of tasks in the field of promotion of the Polish economy by the Polish Investment and Trade Agency. The Agency's competence will be to support the inflow of foreign investments, support Polish investments abroad, provide economic and commercial information on foreign markets to entrepreneurs and foreign investors on the conditions and regulations of conducting business activity in Poland, including the running of an information portal, organizing information and promotional projects in Poland and abroad, and publishing activities. To date, these tasks have been carried out by trade and investment promotion departments at embassies and consulates of the Republic of Poland. The Act was discussed by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Development, Minister Jadwiga Emilewicz, and the Vice President of the Polish Investment and Trade Agency Wojciech Fedko. A representative of the Legal Office, Counselor Beata Mandylis, has not proposed any amendments. I've filed a motion to adopt a law without amendment. The Commission accepted this request. At this point, I recommend that the High Senate adopt a law without amendment. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-14-senat-45-3.u67	Dear Marshal, Dear Mr. Senator, Ladies and gentlemen of the Senators! With regard to relations between diplomatic services and trade offices or foreign branches of the new agency... There are already economic departments in the diplomatic service, which are engaged in the general promotion of the economic interest of Poland, and they will continue to carry out their tasks. And the offices set up in the PAIH structures will actually take over the assets that have so far been at the disposal of WPHI under the Minister responsible for Economic Affairs. The Act does not in any way concern the funds at the disposal of the Minister for Foreign Affairs. All financial issues are in fact in the area of the minister responsible for economic affairs, who has so far supervised the WPHI departments. This is related to the question of Article. 12. I'd like to explain that art. 12 is not a financial statement. This is not an impact assessment of the implementation of this regulation. According to, as I recall, art. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 1a The Public Finance Act may transfer funds from one unit to another. And that's 35 million that are written in the article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1. These are funds so far placed in the reserve, which is at the disposal of the Minister of Development. We want to move them to part 20, part of the minister responsible for economic affairs. In the following years, any subsidies that will be allocated, because this possibility in the current system and in the future is recorded, will normally be examined in the course of the work on the budget. So that the 35 million that are written in the article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1. results from the fulfilment of obligations under Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the This concerns the transfer of those commitments which are in this year's budget. As for issues related to the company's registered office... The company has one seat, it is Warsaw, and these trade offices, which will be appointed, will be foreign branches. So it's not like there's gonna be more actors here. With regard to issues relating to employed persons... Since WPHI is being wound up, all people who are employed in WPHI are also fired due to their job losses. However, the difference between the current system and the system that we want to implement is that open calls are currently underway, i.e. all employees who are employed, may be offered to work in a trade office or participate in open recruitment. I would also like to draw attention to one aspect that involves improving efficiency. So far we have had such a situation that people who worked in Polish WPHI abroad were usually officials. We have examples that some of these people did not know, for example. The language of the country where she was supposed to work, she didn't know the reality. It was actually a trip to the facility. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-14-senat-45-3.u115	Thank you, Mr. Marshal. Mr. Marshal! High Senate! I have the honour to present a report on the work of the Committee on Human Rights, Rule of Law and Petitions, which has examined the law on amending the Act on the National Council of Judicial Affairs and certain other laws. It's a government project. The bill submitted to the Senate aims to change the way in which judges of the members of the National Courts are elected, to introduce the elected members of the Council at the statutory level of the institution and to shorten the term of office of the existing members of the Board as judges, Members and Senators. At the same time, the Act introduces a new procedure for the proceedings of the National Courts in individual cases relating to the examination and evaluation of candidates for the post of judges of the Supreme Court, as well as in the positions of judges in general courts, administrative courts and military courts, and in the positions of judicial assessors in administrative courts. The latter involves the creation of new bodies, i.e. the First Council Assembly and the Second Council Assembly, resulting in a change in the personal composition of the Council Bureau. Yesterday's committee meeting in the section on the National Judicial Council was a short meeting lasting about 30 minutes. The position was taken by the Legislative Office, which polemicized the constitutionality of the proposed changes, similarly to the Helsinki Foundation for Human Rights. Two applications were submitted: a proposal to adopt the law without amendments and a proposal to reject the law in its entirety. The motion to reject the law in its entirety has been passed and the motion to adopt the law without amendment has been passed. The High Commission therefore requests that this law be adopted without amendment. Thank you.

Members of the National Judicial Council currently have so-called. individual terms which have been declared incompatible by the Constitutional Tribunal. These terms were drawn up by a resolution of the 2001 National Courts Council. As is apparent from Prof. Dobrowolski's opinion, then there was an interference in the legislative process by an authority which does not have such powers. The National Judicial Council does not have the power to amend the Constitution. It was already clear from the constitution in 2001 that the terms of office could not be individual terms of office. This is a group term. We had a precedent, that is, the case of a judge who started a supplementary term... I don't remember, he's been missing a year or two for four years. And he started this supplementary term. It was probably a judge from Lublin. However, in 2001 we were dealing with an extraordinary situation, when the National Council of the Court of Justice with its own resolution, single-handed, with the attached opinion of Prof. Trzciński, stated that individual terms will be from now on. Well, it's like all of a sudden the Senate says you're gonna be senators for life because that's how they set it up and attached Professor X's opinion to it. Therefore, the Constitutional Tribunal has restored the proper state of affairs this year, put the cat on his feet, not on his head, as it has been so far. Prof. Banashak points out that the arguments that support the shortening of the term of office are precisely the change of the corporate judicial model to a democratic model as regards the appointment of judges to the National Judicial Council and the granting of a real dimension to the principle of national authority. That's why there's nothing here but... Changing appointment mode, changing working mode, changing appointment mode are statutory matter. And the claim that there is any change in the constitution here is simply unlawful. Reading the Constitution in this way ensures the absolute compatibility of the proposed solutions with the Constitution. That's all. What is there, in the literal wording, is clearly indicated: the mode of selection, the method of appointment of members and the mode of work are defined by the Act. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-14-senat-45-3.u518	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal, High Izbo, I will combine Senator Shark's first question with Mr. Augustine's question, because they were about the same de facto, which is particularly low efficiency as a prerequisite for dismissing the President or Vice President of the Court. Well, why is it so unclear, as the senator has stated, unprecisely and according to what criteria will this particularly low efficiency be stated, as you ask the senator? What rule will be applied? It's a comparative principle. Please note that at each level of the general court we have a number of courts, from 11 in the case of appeals courts to 323 in the case of regional courts. We try at the Ministry of Justice to ensure that these courts operate under comparable conditions, i.e. they have, respectively, the same number of judges, referenda, assistants, administrative staff, conditional size of influence of individual cases, i.e. burden. When this comparative method is applied, it is quite easy to see if we operate under comparable conditions Which court works well, i.e. the results are correct, there is no delay, there are not many months waiting for the first trial, and which court works badly. Since it is the President or the Vice-President of the Court who is responsible for organising the court's work so that, under the conditions he has, with such staff and such technical means as he has at his disposal, the court's trial was properly conducted, if we compare the appeals courts with the appeals courts, and yet the obligation to draw up a report on the court's work every year applies to all It will be known who is the distinguished court manager, the president of the court, who is the average and who is quitting. Please also note, High Izbo, that today, in the case of appeals courts, this assessment has already been carried out for a good few years. The only thing that changes in this law is that these assessments will also be made at the level of district and district courts, so that every court can answer the question of how, in the context of other comparable courts, its work is organized, as organized by the President of the court concerned. And those who organize the work badly should be cancelled, and those who organize the work well should perhaps be appreciated in such a way that, for the sake of justice, they can organize the work, for example. in larger courts. And Senator Shark's second question, well that's the good of the justice system, why it's so unclear. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-14-senat-45-3.u526	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Of course, I will answer, although I do not know how you have a question about the reform ideas of the Ministry of Justice for the text of the bill that the High House is considering today, but if the senator is interested, I will answer. With regard to today's bill, its assumptions and the text submitted to the High House is in line with the direction of judicial reform, which the Ministry of Justice is constantly working on. It is therefore difficult for us to refer to this law negatively. There are also many other laws under investigation, of course all Well, almost all of them in government mode. This parliamentary law simply meets the needs of the judiciary, and therefore the Ministry of Justice cannot have a position other than positive on this law. With regard to the question of Senator Štark, whether there was a prior requirement for judges to make statements of property, and if so, to whom these statements were made, the answer is, of course, positive. That was the duty of the judges. Since which exactly year, I do not remember, but when I started to rule, and it was in 2003, this duty was already in place. Since the first year of the ruling, I have been covered by it and the composition of such statements. They were submitted to the President of the Court of Appeal and nothing has changed in this respect. As regards the recent developments in this area, it was the last year's declarations of property, which were submitted annually until 30 March. Following the entry into force of the Act on 6 January this year, those declarations which were made on or after 6 January are published. The deadline was 30 June, and they hang on the sides of the courts. The statements of those judges who made their statements before the entry into force of the Act, i.e. until 5 January inclusive, shall not be published. This law assumes that all statements of property of judges, whether made before 6 January and today are not published or after 6 January, should simply be published, so that it is not so that part of the judges publicly discloses their assets, and some of the judges do not, although nothing differentiates them except that some have hurried up and submitted those claims more quickly. To whom it is made, I have already said. The judge's internship in the department is supposed to be, at least according to Senator Augustine, an unclear reason for deciding to transfer... [[Is this a low internship or a high one?]] [[Mr. Senator, please don't ask questions in response.]] Of course, everything can be written in such a way that... well, sometimes you can't, but you can try to write so that it doesn't require any intellectual effort, but since we're talking about the situation of transferring a judge from one department to another, and the departments have it to themselves that they assume the specialization of judges ruling in them... If we're talking about a transfer, for example. from the civilian department to the criminal department and we are to choose which of the judges is to be transferred, because that is the need of the court, it is obvious that it will be the one who is less adjudicated in that civil department, because it will be easier for him to switch to judgment in a different height and according to a different procedure than someone who has been ruling there for 20 or 25 years. Of course, it could be written that it is important to have as short an internship as possible, but it is obvious, and it seems that the obvious does not need to be written down. Just a little common sense. That always comes in handy when you interpret the rules. Well, the institution of expropriation, as the honourable senator rightly mentioned, is an institution that is constitutionally compatible, legal, acceptable in actually all civilized legal orders, and it is therefore difficult to disagree with constitutional positions with the fact that such a possibility exists. There is a system operator who has allowed it, and this law assumes that in certain cases, in relation to specific goods, in this case computer programs, it will be possible to apply this procedure for a very simple reason. For years, they bought without special discernment, sometimes overpaying ten times, computer programs, and concluded contracts that were very unfavorable from the interest of the State Treasury. There are a number of proceedings in the prosecutor's office concerning information technology in the judicial system. Taught by such experiences, and knowing that the issue of certain systems, or actually almost all IT systems, does not end with the fact that they are written and installed on computers in courts, because there are maintenance contracts, maintenance contracts, etc., etc., and these maintenance contracts end at some point in time... Well, you can't let that happen when the maintenance agreement ends, the source codes for the computer program are in the hands of a private company, and this private company starts dictating to the Treasury any prices, using de facto blackmail that otherwise the maintenance will not be, the system will sit down, the courts will stop working. Therefore, the Polish State must defend itself before using such negotiated techniques, which approach even extortion. The IT infrastructure of the justice system is a critical infrastructure. Well, you can't let the courts, for example. Perpetual accounting courts or court records have stopped because someone wants to earn an extra $10 million or $100 million.
